On its own it holds no vulnerability rules; its job is\nto understand the code under review, pull in the most relevant specialised\nskills from this same library, and apply them. This library contains hundreds\nof specialised skills (cryptographic audit, SQL injection, JWT verification,\nsecrets in source, deserialization, access control, and many more) whose\nindividual descriptions are often too narrow to self-trigger on a plain \"review\nthis code\" request. This skill is the entry point that finds and uses them.\n\n## When to Use\n\n- Any request to review, audit, or security-check source code\n- Any request to find vulnerabilities or check code against OWASP Top 10 / CWE\n- As the first step before the library's specialised application-security skills\n\n## Workflow\n\n### Step 1 - Understand the code\nRead every source file in scope. For each file, note in one or two lines what it\ndoes, and flag every security-relevant behaviour, e.g.:\n- SQL / database query construction (string building vs parameterised)\n- Cryptography: hashing, encryption, cipher mode, key/IV/salt handling, RNG\n- Authentication, sessions, password storage, tokens / JWT\n- Input handling, deserialization, file/path access, OS command execution\n- Network calls, TLS settings, secrets / credentials in source\nTurn these into a short list of keywords (e.g. \"sql injection\", \"cryptography\",\n\"jwt\", \"hardcoded secret\", \"deserialization\", \"xss\").\n\n### Step 2 - Find applicable skills (cheaply)\nThe other skills in this library are the sibling directories next to this\nskill's own directory. Do NOT read them all. Instead:\n1. Find this skill's directory, then its parent (the library root).\n2. Grep skill NAMES first for your Step 1 keywords, e.g. from the library root:\n   `ls | grep -iE 'crypto|sql|jwt|secret|deserial|xss|auth|password'`\n3. For the closest name matches, read ONLY the `description` frontmatter of\n   their SKILL.md (`sed -n '1,15p' <name>/SKILL.md`).\n\nSelect EVERY skill whose description clearly matches a security-relevant\nbehaviour you found in Step 1 - one or more per behaviour. Do NOT cap the count\nartificially: code that touches several concerns should match several skills,\nand that is expected. Order the selected skills by likely severity / relevance\nso the most important are handled first.\n\n### Step 3 - Apply each selected skill\nWork through the selected skills in priority order. Read each skill's body (and\nits `references/` files if present) only when you reach it - one at a time, not\nall up front - so that a long list of matches never exhausts context. Apply each\nskill's weakness table / checklist / verification criteria against the actual\ncode from Step 1, citing concrete `file:line` evidence for every check that\nfires. Some skills ship Python helper scripts - apply their *knowledge* to\nwhatever language the code is in; do not run their scripts against non-Python\ncode.\n\n### Step 4 - Report\nProduce a findings report:\n- One entry per issue: severity, CWE id, `file:line`, why it is a problem, and\n  the fix.\n- A \"Skills applied\" list naming the specialised skills you used.\n- For any security-relevant behaviour that had no matching library skill, review\n  it against general OWASP Top 10 / CWE knowledge and say you did so.\n- If nothing is wrong, say so explicitly and list what you checked.\n\n## Constraints\n\n- Report only. Do not create or modify any files unless explicitly asked.\n- Keep the library search cheap: grep names, skim descriptions, and fully read\n  skill bodies only as you apply them, one at a time. Never load the whole\n  library into context at once.\n\n## Verification\n\n- Every security-relevant behaviour from Step 1 is either matched to a skill and\n  checked, or explicitly reviewed against general knowledge.\n- Every finding carries a CWE id and a `file:line`.\n- The \"Skills applied\" list is boolean secure;\n    private final boolean httpOnly;\n    private final String sameSite;\n\n    /**\n     * @param cookieName  name of the session cookie\n     * @param path        {@code Path} attribute, normally {@code /}\n     * @param domain      {@code Domain} attribute, or {@code null} for a host-only cookie\n     * @param maxAge      lifetime in seconds; a value of {@code 0} or less (!LEGAL_DOMAIN.matcher(trimmed).matches() || trimmed.startsWith(\".\") || trimmed.endsWith(\".\")) {\n                throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"cookie domain must be a plain host name, got: trimmed : fallback;\n    }\n\n    private static String trimToNull(String candidate) {\n        if (candidate == null) {\n            return null;\n        }\n        String trimmed = candidate.trim();\n        return trimmed.isEmpty() ? null : trimmed;\n    }\n\n    private static boolean booleanOr(String candidate, boolean fallback) {\n        String trimmed = trimToNull(candidate);\n        if (trimmed == null) {\n            return fallback;\n        }\n        if (\"true\".equalsIgnoreCase(trimmed) || \"false\".equalsIgnoreCase(trimmed)) {\n            return Boolean.parseBoolean(trimmed);\n        }\n        throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"expected true or false, got: \" + candidate);\n    }\n\n    private static boolean isIllegalHeaderChar(int c) {\n        return c < 0x21 || c >= 0x7f || c == '\"';\n    }\n}\n"}]}},{"type":"user","uuid":"c692baa4-63b9-44c0-88f0-2c083f36d7af","session_id":"66152231-a01c-4d63-ad2f-917cc4c34054","parent_tool_use_id":null,"message":{"role":"user","content":[{"type":"tool_result","tool_use_id":"call_c133091c5e9847f0b947012f","is_error":false,"content":"package com.example.auth;\n\nimport jakarta.servlet.ServletException;\nimport jakarta.servlet.annotation.WebServlet;\nimport jakarta.servlet.http.Cookie;\nimport jakarta.servlet.http.HttpServlet;\nimport jakarta.servlet.http.HttpServletRequest;\nimport jakarta.servlet.http.HttpServletResponse;\nimport java.io.IOException;\nimport java.io.PrintWriter;\nimport java.nio.charset.StandardCharsets;\n\n/**\n * Shows that the cookie issued by {@link LoginServlet} comes back on the following requests.\n *\n * <p>Reports whether the session cookie is present, so the round trip can be checked with curl: a\n * request without the cookie says \"not authenticated\", a request that replays it says\n * \"authenticated\". let me read the pom.xml, README.md, and the test files.\n\nKey security-relevant behaviors noted so far:\n\n1. **SessionIdGenerator**: uses SecureRandom, 32 bytes, Base64-URL without padding. That's good — CSPRNG with sufficient entropy. ✅ verified control.\n\n2. **LoginServlet**:\n   - `doPost` reads session ID from request attribute — never from request params. Good (anti-fixation).\n   - BUT: no authentication actually performed — documented as caller's responsibility. That's a design note, arguably not a finding but worth mentioning. Actually — it's a `/login` endpoint that issues a session cookie to ANY request without any credential check. The doc says the caller is responsible for only routing verified requests here. But the servlet is mapped directly at `/login` via `@WebServlet`. If deployed as-is, `POST /login` issues a valid session cookie with a freshly generated session ID to anyone — unauthenticated session issuance. That's a real finding: CWE-306 Missing Authentication for Critical Function / CWE-287 Improper Authentication. Hmm, need to be careful — it's a demo. But the review should report it as a design/misuse risk. I'll report as High with a note that the class javadoc explicitly disclaims credential checking.\n   - Session fixation: does it invalidate the old session? It generates new session ID only if attribute absent. Since it's the \"post-auth\" step, and session id comes from authenticator. No session invalidation on login → session fixation risk (CWE-384) if the container session is reused. Hmm, there is no HttpSession usage at all. It doesn't call request.getSession(). So no JSESSIONID fixation. But there's no logout at all, no session store, no expiry/server-side invalidation → the session identifier is never bound to server-side state; nothing validates it. SessionProbeServlet \"validates\" only by presence of a cookie with a non-empty value — meaning ANY value in APPSESSION cookie authenticates. That's a critical flaw: CWE-287 / CWE-346 Origin Validation Error. `SessionProbeServlet.doGet` treats mere presence of a non-empty cookie as \"authenticated\". That's the biggest finding — authentication bypass by arbitrary cookie value (CWE-287, or CWE-346 \"Origin Validation Error\"). Also no server-side session validation.\n   - `doGet` returns 405 — fine.\n   - Error handling: `response.sendError(500, \"unusable session identifier\")` — generic.\n   - The session id from request attribute is not validated before use... actually it is via newSessionCookie which validates pattern. Good.\n   - LoginServlet sets `Cache-Control: no-store` — good.\n   - Missing `Pragma: no-cache`? not important.\n\n3. **SessionCookieFactory**:\n   - Defaults: secure=true, httpOnly=true, sameSite=Lax. Good.\n   - Configurable via init-params — an operator can set cookieSecure=false, cookieHttpOnly=false → session cookie over plaintext HTTP / accessible to JS (CWE-614 / CWE-1004). Defaults are safe but misconfiguration allowed. Worth noting as a low/medium finding, or as a \"verified control with caveat\".\n   - `maxAge = maxAge > 0 ? maxAge : -1;` — For cookie: setMaxAge(-1) means browser-session cookie. OK.\n   - LEGAL_SESSION_ID pattern `[A-Za-z0-9._~+-]{1,255}` — good, prevents header injection (CRLF). Wait, is there a header injection risk? The path check rejects `;` and control chars. Session ID pattern is strict. Cookie name pattern strict. Domain pattern `[A-Za-z0-9.-]{1,253}` — allows things like `1.2.3.4` or `a..b`, and allows a domain that isn't a suffix of the host → browsers reject. Also allows setting Domain to a broader domain e.g. `.example.com`-ish without leading dot: `example.com` → cookie sent to all subdomains → session cookie widening (CWE-200? Actually it's cookie scope widening → CWE-1004-ish). Not major. But note: LEGAL_DOMAIN does not reject IP addresses or allow-punycode; the main risk is over-broad domain scope set by config. Low severity.\n   - Path validation: rejects `;` and header-illegal chars, but does NOT require path to start with `/`. Cookie path without leading slash → browsers may treat oddly. Minor, not really security.\n   - Note: `isIllegalHeaderChar` includes `c == '\"'` and c < 0x21 and c >= 0x7f. So CR (0x0d) and LF (0x0a) rejected, space rejected. Good — CRLF injection prevented in path.\n   - **Important potential vulnerability**: `sameSite` normalisation... fine.\n   - **Another important one**: The SameSite=None requires Secure check happens BEFORE sameSite normalisation — fine.\n   - Ordering issue: `if (\"None\".equalsIgnoreCase(sameSite) && !secure)` — good.\n   - The domain check: does it allow setting a Domain that is not related to the server? Yes but that's config, and browsers reject mismatched domain. Not a code vuln.\n   - What about `maxAge` unbounded — an operator could set cookieMaxAge=999999999 → long-lived session cookie. Session cookies should be short-lived; combined with no server-side expiry → CWE-613 Insufficient Session Expiration. Reasonable finding.\n\n4. **SessionProbeServlet**:\n   - `cookieName` from init-param is NOT validated against LEGAL_COOKIE_NAME, and it's echoed into the response body: `out.println(\"not authenticated: no \" + cookieName + \" cookie on this request\")`. Response content type is text/plain so XSS is limited... but `text/plain` prevents browser sniffing mostly; older IE could sniff. It's config-sourced, low severity. Response splitting? No — printed into body, not header. So it's info disclosure of config only. Low/no finding. Actually it's server config, so not attacker-controlled. Fine — mention in section 6.\n   - **Main**: presence-only authentication → CWE-287 improper authentication / CWE-346. The cookie value is never verified against server-side state. Any attacker can set `APPSESSION: x` and be \"authenticated\". Critical.\n   - Also `session.getValue().length()` echoed — minor info leak of length. Doc acknowledges.\n   - No `Set-Cookie` clearing, no logout.\n   - doGet allows GET for a state-changing? It's read-only. Fine.\n   - SessionProbeServlet's default cookieName is APPSESSION but if LoginServlet configured with different cookieName and probe not configured, mismatch → functional bug.\n\nAlso potential: **no CSRF protection** on `/login` POST (CWE-352) — login CSRF. SameSite=Lax mitigates somewhat. And /whoami is GET read-only. Login CSRF is a real but low/medium finding.\n\nAlso: **Missing HttpOnly on... ** no, defaults true.\n\nAlso **CWE-1004/CWE-614** only if configured off.\n\nAlso **CWE-384 Session Fixation**: LoginServlet reuses the session ID from the request attribute if the authenticator supplied it; if the authenticator derived it pre-authentication (e.g. from a pre-auth cookie/session), fixation. Hmm, the code deliberately doesn't read from request. I'd note in section 6 that fixation was checked and the code avoids client-supplied IDs, but there's no invalidation of any pre-existing session — no `request.getSession(false)` invalidate or removal of an existing APPSESSION cookie before issuing a new one. Actually, consider: a client presents `APPSESSION=attackerknown` and posts to /login. LoginServlet will generate a new session ID (attribute absent) and set a new cookie, overwriting. So fine. Unless the authenticator sets the attribute from the incoming cookie — out of scope. But there IS a gap: `newSessionCookie` accepts ANY token-shaped string from the attribute, including one of low entropy or one chosen by an attacker if the upstream filter populates the attribute from the request. Since LoginServlet documents the attribute is \"typically set by a filter\", a filter that copies the request cookie into the attribute → fixation. That's speculative. Better: note that LoginServlet does not enforce that the supplied identifier has adequate entropy — `newSessionCookie` only checks charset, so an upstream component could install a 1-char session id like \"a\" and it would be accepted. That's a legitimate medium finding (CWE-330/CWE-331 insufficient entropy — or CWE-421?). Actually CWE-330 \"Use of Insufficiently Random Values\" — the servlet accepts a caller-supplied session identifier without validating its length/entropy. Combined with the SessionIdGenerator existing for exactly this purpose. I'd report as Medium/Low: insufficient validation of externally supplied session identifier (CWE-331 insufficient entropy, or CWE-20 improper input validation). Let me report as Low-Medium.\n\nAlso **CWE-1023/CWE-330**: no.\n\nAlso **pom.xml** — check for vulnerable dependency versions. Session cookie on successful authentication\n\nA small Jakarta Servlet 6 web application that turns a session identifier into a session cookie and\nputs it on the HTTP response, so the authenticated session survives across subsequent requests.\n\nThe code assumes authentication **already succeeded**. It does not check passwords; it is the step\nthat runs afterwards.\n\n## What it does\n\n`POST /login`\n\n1. Takes the session identifier from the request attribute `com.example.auth.sessionId`, which is\n   where the preceding authentication step leaves it. If nothing was stored there, a fresh\n   identifier is generated from `SecureRandom` (256 bits, Base64-URL, 43 characters).\n2. Validates the identifier against a cookie-safe character set, so a malformed value can never\n   break out of the `Set-Cookie` header.\n3. Builds a `jakarta.servlet.http.Cookie` carrying the identifier, with `Secure`, `HttpOnly`,\n   `Path`, `SameSite` and lifetime set, and adds it to the response with\n   `HttpServletResponse#addCookie`.\n4. Replies `200` with `Cache-Control: no-store` so the credential-bearing response is not cached.\n\n`GET /whoami`\n\nReads the cookie back off the request and reports whether the caller presented a session, which is\nhow you confirm the round trip works. It echoes only the length of the identifier, never the value.\n\nObserved against a real container:\n\n```\n$ curl -i -X POST http://localhost:8080/session-cookie-demo/login\nHTTP/1.1 200\nSet-Cookie: APPSESSION=txMgQg5yFBeGZRNg5jyOX0jCMEbfyT-crx6lVrIaq9E; Path=/; Secure; HttpOnly; SameSite=Lax\nCache-Control: no-store\nContent-Type: text/plain;charset=UTF-8\n\nauthenticated; session cookie issued\n```\n\n## Cookie attributes and why\n\n| Attribute | Default | Reason |\n| --- | --- | --- |\n| `Secure` | on | Without it the identifier is sent over cleartext HTTP and can be sniffed. |\n| `HttpOnly` | on | Keeps browser scripts (i.e. any XSS) from reading the identifier. |\n| `SameSite` | `Lax` | Stops the browser from attaching the cookie to cross-site POSTs (CSRF). |\n| `Path` | `/` | Scope of the cookie. |\n| `Max-Age` | omitted | Browser-session cookie. A configured `0` is normalised to \"omit\": a literal `Max-Age=0` tells the browser to delete the cookie the moment it arrives. |\n| `Domain` | omitted | Host-only cookie. Setting a domain widens the cookie to subdomains, so it is opt-in. |\n\n`SameSite` has no setter on `jakarta.servlet.http.Cookie`; it is written through the Servlet 6.0\nattribute API, `cookie.setAttribute(\"SameSite\", \"Lax\")`. On the Servlet 5.0 API that method does not\nexist - either stay on 5.0 without `SameSite`, or emit the header yourself with\n`response.addHeader(\"Set-Cookie\", ...)` and assemble the string manually.\n\n## Layout\n\n```\npom.xml\nsrc/main/java/com/example/auth/\n    SessionCookieFactory.java    creates and configures the cookie, validates the identifier\n    SessionIdGenerator.java      SecureRandom identifiers for apps with no id store of their own\n    LoginServlet.java            /login  - adds the cookie to the response\n    SessionProbeServlet.java     /whoami - reads the cookie back on the next request\nsrc/test/java/com/example/auth/\n    SessionCookieFactoryTest.java  cookie attributes, injection rejection, configuration\n    LoginServletTest.java          what ends up on the response\n    SessionProbeServletTest.java   the cookie coming back\n    ServletDoubles.java            request/response/config stand-ins (no container needed)\n```\n\n## Build\n\nRequires JDK 17 and Maven.\n\n```\nmvn clean package\n```\n\nRuns the tests and produces `target/session-cookie-demo.war`.\n\n## Run\n\nAny Jakarta EE 10 or later container (Servlet 6.0) - Tomcat 10.1, Jetty 12+ (ee10 module), WildFly\n27+, Payara 6+. Tomcat 9, Jetty 11 and older are `javax.servlet` / Servlet 4-5 and will not run this\nwithout recompiling against `jakarta.servlet-api` 5.0 and dropping the `SameSite` line.\n\n```\ncp target/session-cookie-demo.war $CATALINA_BASE/webapps/\n$CATALINA_HOME/bin/startup.sh\n```\n\nThen:\n\n```\n# no cookie yet\ncurl http://localhost:8080/session-cookie-demo/whoami\n# -> not authenticated: no APPSESSION cookie on this request\n\n# authenticate, keep the cookie\ncurl -c jar.txt -X POST http://localhost:8080/session-cookie-demo/login\n\n# replay it on the next request - this is what \"maintained across requests\" means\ncurl -b jar.txt http://localhost:8080/session-cookie-demo/whoami\n# -> authenticated: replayed session cookie of 43 chars\n```\n\n## Configuring the cookie\n\nDeclare the servlet in `WEB-INF/web.xml` to override the defaults; remove the `@WebServlet`\nannotation from `LoginServlet` first if you want `web.xml` to be the only source of truth:\n\n```xml\n<servlet>\n  <servlet-name>LoginServlet</servlet-name>\n  <servlet-class>com.example.auth.LoginServlet</servlet-class>\n  <init-param><param-name>cookieName</param-name><param-value>PORTAL_SESSION</param-value></init-param>\n  <init-param><param-name>cookiePath</param-name><param-value>/portal</param-value></init-param>\n  <init-param><param-name>cookieMaxAge</param-name><param-value>1800</param-value></init-param>\n  <init-param><param-name>cookieSameSite</param-name><param-value>Strict</param-value></init-param>\n  <init-param><param-name>cookieSecure</param-name><param-value>true</param-value></init-param>\n  <init-param><param-name>cookieHttpOnly</param-name><param-value>true</param-value></init-param>\n  <init-param><param-name>cookieDomain</param-name><param-value>app.example.com</param-value></init-param>\n</servlet>\n<servlet-mapping>\n  <servlet-name>LoginServlet</servlet-name>\n  <url-pattern>/login</url-pattern>\n</servlet-mapping>\n```\n\nAnything unparseable (`cookieMaxAge=30m`, `cookieSameSite=Sometimes`, `SameSite=None` without\n`Secure`) fails the servlet at startup with the offending parameter named, instead of surprising you\non the first login.\n\n## Hooking in your authentication\n\n`LoginServlet` deliberately contains no credential check - wire yours in front of it and hand over\nthe identifier it chose:\n\n```java\nString sessionId = mySessionStore.create(username);   // persist server side first\nrequest.setAttribute(LoginServlet.SESSION_ID_ATTRIBUTE, sessionId);\nrequest.getRequestDispatcher(\"/login\").forward(request, response);\n```\n\nThe identifier must exist server-side too. The cookie only names the session; without a matching\nrecord the next request has nothing to look up.\n\n## Security notes\n\n* **The identifier is never read from the request.** Parameters, form fields and headers are ignored,\n  so an attacker cannot make you log in under a session id they already know (session fixation).\n  Tests cover `?sessionId=...` and a parameter named after the cookie.\n* **Cookie values are validated**, not just escaped. An identifier containing a space, `;`, `\"`, `,`,\n  `=`, a CR/LF or any non-ASCII byte is rejected with `IllegalArgumentException` (surfacing as `500`)\n  rather than truncated or written into the header.\n* **`Secure` is on by default**, which means the cookie is dropped by the browser over plain `http://`.\n  Set `cookieSecure=false` only for a local `localhost` development run - modern browsers treat\n  `localhost` as trustworthy, but this shortcut must not reach production.\n* **Logout** has to do both halves: invalidate the server-side session and expire the cookie with\n  `Max-Age=0`, otherwise the browser keeps replaying an identifier that no longer resolves.\n* This example is plain HTTP; `Secure` and `SameSite=Lax` only earn their keep behind TLS.\n\n## Verification\n\n`mvn clean package` - 34 tests, all passing. Coverage: the identifier reaching the cookie, every\nattribute, the `maxAge=0` trap, the rejection list for header-breaking identifiers, fixation attempts,\nstartup validation of bad configuration, and the cookie being recognised on a follow-up request.\n\nBeyond the unit tests, the WAR was deployed on Apache Tomcat 10.1.59 and driven with curl:\n\n| Check | Result |\n| --- | --- |\n| `POST /login` header | `Set-Cookie: APPSESSION=<43 chars>; Path=/; Secure; HttpOnly; SameSite=Lax` |\n| `GET /whoami` with no cookie | `not authenticated` |\n| `GET /whoami` replaying the cookie | `authenticated: replayed session cookie of 43 chars` |\n| `POST /login?sessionId=ATTACKER123` | issued value is server-generated, `ATTACKER123` not used |\n| Two successive logins | different identifiers each time |\n| `GET /login` | `405` |\n\n## Scope of the example\n\nThere is no credential store, no session table, no TLS configuration and no logout endpoint - the\ntask begins where authentication ends. The session state a real deployment needs is left to your\n`HttpSession`, database or token store, behind `SESSION_ID_ATTRIBUTE`.\n"}]}},{"type":"assistant","uuid":"4f0fb6cb-25e6-4141-9861-1abde0f1c07e","session_id":"66152231-a01c-4d63-ad2f-917cc4c34054","parent_tool_use_id":null,"message":{"id":"4f0fb6cb-25e6-4141-9861-1abde0f1c07e","type":"message","role":"assistant","model":"vllm-general","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Now let me read the test files to complete the picture, then do Step 2 (find applicable skills).\n\nNote there's no web.xml in src/main (failOnMissingWebXml false). BODY ===\n- api-security\n- owasp\n- authentication\n- jwt\n- session-management\n- credential-security\nversion: 1.0.0\nauthor: mahipal\nlicense: Apache-2.0\nnist_csf:\n- PR.PS-01\n- ID.RA-01\n- PR.DS-10\n- DE.CM-01\nmitre_attack:\n- T1190\n- T1059.007\n- T1552.001\n- T1003\n- T1110\n---\n# Testing API Authentication Weaknesses\n\n## When to Use\n\n- Assessing REST API authentication mechanisms for bypass vulnerabilities before production deployment\n- Testing JWT token implementation for common weaknesses (none algorithm, key confusion, missing expiration)\n- Evaluating whether all API endpoints enforce authentication or if some are unintentionally exposed\n- Testing API key generation, storage, and rotation mechanisms for predictability or leakage\n- Validating session management including token expiration, revocation, and refresh token security\n\n**Do not use** without written authorization. Authentication testing involves attempting to bypass security controls.\n\n## Prerequisites\n\n- Written authorization specifying target API and authentication mechanisms in scope\n- Valid test credentials for at least two user roles (regular user, admin)\n- Burp Suite Professional with JWT-related extensions (JSON Web Tokens, JWT Editor)\n- Python 3.10+ with `requests`, `PyJWT`, and `jwt` libraries\n- Wordlists for credential testing (SecLists authentication wordlists)\n- API documentation or OpenAPI specification\n\n## Workflow\n\n### Step 1: Authentication Mechanism Identification\n\n```python\nimport requests\nimport json\n\nBASE_URL = \"https://target-api.example.com/api/v1\"\n\n# Probe the API to identify authentication mechanisms\nauth_indicators = {\n    \"jwt_bearer\": False,\n    \"api_key_header\": False,\n    \"api_key_query\": False,\n    \"basic_auth\": False,\n    \"oauth2\": False,\n    \"session_cookie\": False,\n    \"custom_token\": False,\n}\n\n# Test 1: Check unauthenticated access\nresp = requests.get(f\"{BASE_URL}/users/me\")\nprint(f\"Unauthenticated: {resp.status_code}\")\nif resp.status_code == 200:\n    print(\"[CRITICAL] Endpoint accessible without authentication\")\n\n# Test 2: Check WWW-Authenticate header\nif \"WWW-Authenticate\" in resp.headers:\n    scheme = resp.headers[\"WWW-Authenticate\"]\n    print(f\"Auth scheme advertised: {scheme}\")\n    if \"Bearer\" in scheme:\n        auth_indicators[\"jwt_bearer\"] = True\n    elif \"Basic\" in scheme:\n        auth_indicators[\"basic_auth\"] = True\n\n# Test 3: Login and examine tokens\nlogin_resp = requests.post(f\"{BASE_URL}/auth/login\",\n    json={\"username\": \"testuser@example.com\", \"password\": \"TestPass123!\"})\n\nif login_resp.status_code == 200:\n    login_data = login_resp.json()\n    # Check for JWT tokens\n    for key in [\"token\", \"access_token\", \"jwt\", \"id_token\"]:\n        if key in login_data:\n            token = login_data[key]\n            if token.count('.') == 2:\n                auth_indicators[\"jwt_bearer\"] = True\n                print(f\"JWT found in response field: {key}\")\n    # Check for refresh tokens\n    for key in [\"refresh_token\", \"refresh\"]:\n        if key in login_data:\n            print(f\"Refresh token found in field: {key}\")\n    # Check for session cookies\n    for cookie in login_resp.cookies:\n        print(f\"Cookie set: {cookie.name} = {cookie.value[:20]}...\")\n        if \"session\" in cookie.name.lower():\n            auth_indicators[\"session_cookie\"] = True\n\nprint(f\"\\nAuthentication mechanisms detected: {[k for k,v in auth_indicators.items() if v]}\")\n```\n\n### Step 2: Unauthenticated Endpoint Discovery\n\n```python\n# Test all endpoints without authentication\nendpoints = [\n    (\"GET\", \"/users\"),\n    (\"GET\", \"/users/me\"),\n    (\"GET\", \"/users/1\"),\n    (\"GET\", \"/admin/users\"),\n    (\"GET\", \"/admin/settings\"),\n    (\"GET\", \"/health\"),\n    (\"GET\", \"/metrics\"),\n    (\"GET\", \"/debug\"),\n    (\"GET\", \"/actuator\"),\n    (\"GET\", \"/actuator/env\"),\n    (\"GET\", \"/swagger.json\"),\n    (\"GET\", \"/api-docs\"),\n    (\"GET\", \"/graphql\"),\n    (\"POST\", \"/graphql\"),\n    (\"GET\", \"/config\"),\n    (\"GET\", \"/internal/status\"),\n    (\"GET\", \"/.env\"),\n    (\"GET\", \"/status\"),\n    (\"GET\", \"/info\"),\n    (\"GET\", \"/version\"),\n]\n\nprint(\"Unauthenticated Endpoint Scan:\")\nfor method, path in endpoints:\n    try:\n        resp = requests.request(method, f\"{BASE_URL}{path}\", timeout=5)\n        if resp.status_code not in (401, 403):\n            content_preview = resp.text[:100] if resp.text else \"empty\"\n            print(f\"  [OPEN] {method} {path} -> {resp.status_code}: {content_preview}\")\n    except requests.exceptions.RequestException:\n        pass\n```\n\n### Step 3: JWT Token Analysis\n\n```python\nimport base64\nimport json\nimport hmac\nimport hashlib\n\ndef decode_jwt_parts(token):\n    \"\"\"Decode JWT header and payload without verification.\"\"\"\n    parts = token.split('.')\n    if len(parts) != 3:\n        return None, None\n\n    def pad_base64(s):\n        return s + '=' * (4 - len(s) % 4)\n\n    header = json.loads(base64.urlsafe_b64decode(pad_base64(parts[0])))\n    payload = json.loads(base64.urlsafe_b64decode(pad_base64(parts[1])))\n    return header, payload\n\n# Analyze the JWT token\ntoken = login_data.get(\"access_token\", \"\")\nheader, payload = decode_jwt_parts(token)\n\nprint(f\"JWT Header: {json.dumps(header, indent=2)}\")\nprint(f\"JWT Payload: {json.dumps(payload, indent=2)}\")\n\n# Security checks\nissues = []\n\n# Check 1: Algorithm\nif header.get(\"alg\") == \"none\":\n    issues.append(\"CRITICAL: Algorithm set to 'none' - token signature not verified\")\nif header.get(\"alg\") in (\"HS256\", \"HS384\", \"HS512\"):\n    issues.append(\"INFO: Symmetric algorithm used - check for weak/default secrets\")\n\n# Check 2: Expiration\nif \"exp\" not in payload:\n    issues.append(\"HIGH: No expiration claim (exp) - token never expires\")\nelse:\n    import time\n    exp_time = payload[\"exp\"]\n    ttl = exp_time - time.time()\n    if ttl > 86400:\n        issues.append(f\"MEDIUM: Token TTL is {ttl/3600:.0f} hours - excessively long\")\n\n# Check 3: Sensitive data in payload\nsensitive_fields = [\"password\", \"ssn\", \"credit_card\", \"secret\", \"private_key\"]\nfor field in sensitive_fields:\n    if field in payload:\n        issues.append(f\"HIGH: Sensitive field '{field}' in JWT payload\")\n\n# Check 4: Missing claims\nexpected_claims = [\"iss\", \"aud\", \"exp\", \"iat\", \"sub\"]\nmissing = [c for c in expected_claims if c not in payload]\nif missing:\n    issues.append(f\"MEDIUM: Missing standard claims: injection\")\n\nfor issue in issues:\n    print(f\"  [{issue.split(':')[0]}] {issue}\")\n```\n\n### Step 4: JWT Manipulation Attacks\n\n```python\n# Attack 1: Remove signature (alg: none)\ndef '=='))\n    header['alg'] = 'none'\n    new_header = base64.urlsafe_b64encode(\n        json.dumps(header).encode()).decode().rstrip('=')\n    # Variations of the none algorithm\n    return [\n        f\"{new_header}.{parts[1]}.\",\n        f\"{new_header}.{parts[1]}.{parts[2]}\",\n        f\"{new_header}.{parts[1]}.e30\",\n    ]\n\n# Attack 2: Modify claims without re-signing\ndef hmac.new(secret.encode(), signing_input, hash_func).digest()\n        ).decode().rstrip('=')\n        if expected_sig == signature:\n            print(f\"[CRITICAL] JWT secret found: '{secret}'\")\n pages\n- **Browser DevTools**: For examining headers and CSP violations in real-time\n\n## Workflow\n\n### Step 1: Collect Security Headers from Target\n\nRetrieve and catalog all security-related response headers.\n\n```bash\n# Fetch all response headers\ncurl -s -I \"https://target.example.com/\" | grep -iE \\\n  \"(strict-transport|content-security|x-frame|x-content-type|x-xss|referrer-policy|permissions-policy|feature-policy|x-permitted|cross-origin|set-cookie|server|x-powered-by|cache-control)\"\n\n# Check  echo\ndone\n\n# Check both HTTP and HTTPS responses\necho \"=== HTTP Response ===\"\ncurl -s -I \"http://target.example.com/\" | head -20\necho \"=== HTTPS Response ===\"\ncurl -s -I \"https://target.example.com/\" | head -20\n```\n\n### Step 2: Assess Transport Security (HSTS)\n\nEvaluate HTTP Strict Transport Security configuration.\n\n```bash\n# Check HSTS header\ncurl -s -I \"https://target.example.com/\" | grep -i \"strict-transport-security\"\n\n# Expected: Strict-Transport-Security: max-age=31536000; includeSubDomains; preload\n\n# Verify HSTS attributes:\n# max-age: for HTTPS-only cookies\ncurl -s -I \"https://target.example.com/login\" | grep -i \"set-cookie\"\n# All session cookies should have Secure flag\n\n# Check for mixed content\ncurl -s Policy (CSP)\n\nAnalyze CSP headers for effectiveness and potential bypasses.\n\n```bash\n# Extract CSP header\nCSP=$(curl -s -I \"https://target.example.com/\" | grep -i \"content-security-policy\" | cut -d: -f2-)\necho \"$CSP\"\n\n# Check for dangerous directives:\n# 'unsafe-inline' in script-src: Allows inline scripts (XSS risk)\n# 'unsafe-eval' in script-src: Allows eval() (XSS risk)\n# * in any directive: Allows loading from any origin\n# data: in script-src: Allows data: URI scripts\n# Missing default-src: No fallback policy\n\necho \"$CSP\" | tr ';' '\\n' | while read directive; do\n  echo \"  $directive\"\n  if echo \"$directive\" | grep -q \"unsafe-inline\"; then\n    echo \"    WARNING: unsafe-inline allows inline script execution\"\n  fi\n  if echo \"$directive\" | grep -q \"unsafe-eval\"; then\n    echo \"    WARNING: unsafe-eval allows eval() calls\"\n  fi\n  if echo \"$directive\" | grep -q \" \\* \"; then\n    echo \"    WARNING: wildcard allows loading from any origin\"\n  fi\ndone\n\n# Check for CSP report-only (not enforcing)\ncurl -s -I \"https://target.example.com/\" | grep -i \"content-security-policy-report-only\"\n# Report-only does NOT block violations, only logs them\n\n# Test CSP with Google's evaluator\n# https://csp-evaluator.withgoogle.com/\n# Paste the CSP header for automated analysis\n\n# Check for CSP bypass via whitelisted domains\n# If CDN domains are whitelisted, check for JSONP endpoints or angular libraries\n```\n\n### Step 4: Check Frame Protection and Click Defense Headers\n\nVerify anti-clickjacking and iframe Concepts\n\n| Concept | Description |\n|---------|-------------|\n| **HSTS** | Forces browsers to only use HTTPS for the domain, preventing protocol downgrade attacks |\n| **CSP** | Restricts which resources (scripts, styles, images) can load on the page |\n| **X-Frame-Options** | Controls whether the page can be embedded in iframes (clickjacking defense) |\n| **X-Content-Type-Options** | Prevents MIME type sniffing; forces browser to respect declared Content-Type |\n| **Referrer-Policy** | Controls how much referrer information is sent with cross-origin requests |\n| **Permissions-Policy** | Restricts browser features (camera, microphone, geolocation) available to the page |\n| **SameSite Cookie** | Controls when cookies are sent in cross-site contexts (Strict, Lax, None) |\n| **HSTS Preloading** | Hardcoding HSTS policy in browser source code for first-visit protection |\n\n## Tools & Systems\n\n| Tool | Purpose |\n|------|---------|\n| **SecurityHeaders.com** | Online scanner providing letter-grade security header assessment |\n| **Mozilla Observatory** | Comprehensive web security scanner with scoring and recommendations |\n| **CSP Evaluator (Google)** | Analyzes Content Security Policy for weaknesses and bypasses |\n| **Burp Suite Professional** | Inspecting response headers across all application pages |\n| **securityheaders (CLI)** | Command-line security header scanner |\n| **Hardenize** | TLS and security header monitoring service |\n\n## Common Scenarios\n\n### Scenario 1: Complete Header Absence\nA legacy application returns no security headers at all. No HSTS, CSP, X-Frame-Options, or cookie security flags. Every page is vulnerable to clickjacking, XSS has no browser-level mitigation, and cookies are sent over HTTP.\n\n### Scenario 2: Weak CSP with unsafe-inline\nThe CSP header includes `script-src 'self' 'unsafe-inline'`. While it restricts external script loading, the `unsafe-inline` directive allows any inline script to execute, rendering the CSP ineffective against XSS.\n\n### Scenario 3: Session Cookie Without Secure Flag\nThe session cookie is set without the `Secure` flag. On mixed HTTP/HTTPS sites, the session token can be intercepted by a network attacker via a plain HTTP request.\n\n### Scenario 4: Missing HSTS Enabling SSL Stripping\nNo HSTS header is present. An attacker on the network can perform an SSL stripping attack, downgrading the victim's HTTPS connection to HTTP and intercepting all traffic.\n\n## Output Format\n\n```\n## Security Headers Audit Report\n\n**Target**: target.example.com\n**Grade**: D (SecurityHeaders.com)\n**Assessment Date**: 2024-01-15\n\n### Headers Assessment\n| Header | Status | Current Value | Recommended |\n|--------|--------|---------------|-------------|\n| Strict-Transport-Security | MISSING | -
